# All current broader source findings
This is a source-backed research catalogue, compiled October 29, 2026. The evaluation chat began September 2, 2026. Coverage is against the frozen registers listed in PUBLICATION.json; it is not a claim to have replayed every historical chat turn or independently checked every primary source.

The entries overlap. A file discrepancy, a reported event, an allegation, a missing input, a model-dependent result and a failed control are different kinds of record. Counts are not independent anomalies or probabilities of conspiracy. Source-level checking, historical authenticity, physical replication, intent and cause remain separate. Read each note with its limits and the linked owning record. Existing stable IDs are retained; this is navigation, not a replacement authority.

External citations are inherited from the held research. They are not represented as freshly fetched or independently authenticated in this compilation. Local citations point to pinned entry copies and, where resolvable, their owning reports. Missing or unavailable originals remain open.

This appendix preserves all 355 entries in the pinned source-findings-v131 cohort. Some record additional evidence; others qualify a premise, identify a search route, document a negative result or correct our method. These are not 355 established historical anomalies.

<a id="family-s"></a>
## Initial CIA/FBI and oversight findings

<a id="s001"></a>
### S001 — A specific hold instruction, not merely a failure to pass a message

The DOJ OIG reconstructs a CIA-system draft CIR carrying Mihdhar visa/travel information, a same-day hold note attributed to the CIA deputy chief, and a later unanswered query about sending/remaking it. It reports that the draft was not formally disseminated to its FBI addressees.

**Record type / scope:** Source content checked; historical inference explicitly bounded

**Context / limit:** FBI personnel detailed to the CIA had accessed the visa cables/draft. Therefore “no FBI employee knew” is broader than the reported formal-dissemination failure. Witnesses generally reported no recollection; the hold purpose was not established.

**Next record/test:** Acquire the exact draft/routing record and distinguish individual access from dissemination to FBI operational addressees.

**Citations:** [pinned entry S001](CITATIONS.md#local-1a524e9b14c50c0d); [DOJ OIG chapter5.htm](https://oig.justice.gov/sites/default/files/archive/special/0506/chapter5.htm); Locator: Chapter V III.A.4; derived blocks P089–P099; IV.A P439

<a id="s002"></a>
### S002 — Conflicting contemporary handling accounts remain side by side

The OIG describes an internal CIA cable saying the visa documents had been passed to the FBI, approximately three hours after the hold note. It found no witness or additional document corroborating that transfer.

**Record type / scope:** Source content checked; historical inference explicitly bounded

**Context / limit:** An incorrect statement, an undocumented transfer or other process failure are competing possibilities; the report did not determine why the cable said this. CIA/FBI interviews and access logs do not certify receipt by the named operational addressees.

**Next record/test:** Match the original cable statement to a recipient acknowledgment or a complete negative search with scope.

**Citations:** [pinned entry S002](CITATIONS.md#local-2e32235ca036d3b3); [DOJ OIG chapter5.htm](https://oig.justice.gov/sites/default/files/archive/special/0506/chapter5.htm); Locator: III.A.6; blocks P104–P110

<a id="s003"></a>
### S003 — Later oversight nonproduction is a distinct event

The OIG states that it discovered the CIR title in February 2004 in a list of documents accessed by FBI detailees, after neither it nor the CIA OIG had previously obtained the draft; follow-up work delayed the review.

**Record type / scope:** Source content checked; historical inference explicitly bounded

**Context / limit:** The OIG lacked independent access to CIA databases. The published account establishes a late-discovery problem, not the reason the document was absent or a demonstrated connection to the 2000 hold decision.

**Next record/test:** Compare the exact request terms, searches and production chronology rather than treating every omission as destruction.

**Citations:** [pinned entry S003](CITATIONS.md#local-bbcc2e631d650cf5); [DOJ OIG chapter5.htm](https://oig.justice.gov/sites/default/files/archive/special/0506/chapter5.htm); Locator: I–II background; blocks P021–P028

<a id="s004"></a>
### S004 — FBI-asset tenancy and conflicting disclosures

The OIG reports that Hazmi and Mihdhar boarded with an FBI informational asset in 2000. It records inconsistent accounts about whether the asset told his handler about them, what identities were supplied and what follow-up occurred.

**Record type / scope:** Source content checked; historical inference explicitly bounded

**Context / limit:** The asset and retired handler were not interviewed directly by OIG, although the handler had spoken to FBI supervisors and congressional investigators. OIG notes an inconclusive polygraph and the San Diego FBI conclusion that the asset was not complicit. None should be dropped.

**Next record/test:** Cross-reference dated contact notes against each later account and identify which assertions are independently documented.

**Citations:** [pinned entry S004](CITATIONS.md#local-70a6ca7ffb6f2b8a); [DOJ OIG chapter5.htm](https://oig.justice.gov/sites/default/files/archive/special/0506/chapter5.htm); Locator: III.B.3; blocks P199–P211; notes 130–135 at P653–P658

<a id="s005"></a>
### S005 — Documentation criticism differs from a proved policy violation

The OIG criticizes thin annual bona-fides documentation and missing retrievable information, while saying no specific FBI rule required the handler to investigate or record the identities of these boarders.

**Record type / scope:** Source content checked; historical inference explicitly bounded

**Context / limit:** The lack of a specific boarder rule and disagreement among other agents are contrary to a blanket “proved rule violation” characterization. The policy review still identifies a concrete oversight weakness.

**Next record/test:** Inspect the actual evaluation and policy texts, separating a missing policy from failure to follow an existing one.

**Citations:** [pinned entry S005](CITATIONS.md#local-8907010efd404fea); [DOJ OIG chapter5.htm](https://oig.justice.gov/sites/default/files/archive/special/0506/chapter5.htm); Locator: IV.B.3; blocks P511–P527; conclusions P594–P596

<a id="s006"></a>
### S006 — A documented payment/closure lead without an established purpose

OIG note 133 reports that the initially unpaid asset received $100,000 and was closed as an asset in July 2003.

**Record type / scope:** Source content checked; historical inference explicitly bounded

**Context / limit:** The note does not establish what the payment compensated, who approved it or why the asset was closed. A hush-payment or protection theory is a hypothesis, not a result of reading the amount.

**Next record/test:** Obtain the payment/closeout authorization before assigning a motive.

**Citations:** [pinned entry S006](CITATIONS.md#local-e8e95b3952d27fe4); [DOJ OIG chapter5.htm](https://oig.justice.gov/sites/default/files/archive/special/0506/chapter5.htm); Locator: Note 133; derived block P656

<a id="s007"></a>
### S007 — Specific assistance and a repayment that changes the financial characterization

The 2016 EC reports translation, travel, lodging and financing assistance and labels coincident transfers anomalous. Separately, OIG reports a same-day reimbursement of the first rent/deposit advance; note 129 gives $1,558 and a nearby $9,900 deposit. The 2025 court also describes repayment.

**Record type / scope:** Source content checked; historical inference explicitly bounded

**Context / limit:** A reimbursed advance is not automatically an unrepaid gift or proof of the original source of the hijackers’ funds. Repayment does not erase logistical facilitation. The EC’s broader anomalous-transfer claim cannot be resolved using one summarized transaction.

**Next record/test:** Match the actual debits, credits, timing, amounts and beneficial sources across the whole claimed transfer set.

**Citations:** [pinned entry S007](CITATIONS.md#local-17463ffaa08d92b9); [www.govinfo.gov — GOVPUB-J1_14-PURL-gpo159739.pdf](https://www.govinfo.gov/content/pkg/GOVPUB-J1_14-PURL-gpo159739/pdf/GOVPUB-J1_14-PURL-gpo159739.pdf); Locator: Physical PDF p9 / internal p8 OIG III.B.2 P196 and note 129 P652 Court physical p21

<a id="s008"></a>
### S008 — Restaurant accounts conflict, with limits on what the witness could observe

The EC contrasts Bayoumi’s claimed overhearing of Arabic while leaving with BIN DON’s account of a front-window position and approach at a distance inconsistent with first hearing the speech. BIN DON is reported not to understand Arabic.

**Record type / scope:** Source content checked; historical inference explicitly bounded

**Context / limit:** A witness can report position and movement without knowing the Arabic conversation or an earlier plan. The original statements and their interview dates/references are not reproduced in full. Do not count their later summaries as new witnesses.

**Next record/test:** Compare complete statements, opportunity to observe, sequence of questions and any contemporary account.

**Citations:** [pinned entry S008](CITATIONS.md#local-8b0beeb1de29bb2c); [www.govinfo.gov — GOVPUB-J1_14-PURL-gpo159739.pdf](https://www.govinfo.gov/content/pkg/GOVPUB-J1_14-PURL-gpo159739/pdf/GOVPUB-J1_14-PURL-gpo159739.pdf); Locator: Physical pp9–10 / internal pp8–9

<a id="s009"></a>
### S009 — Later court record adds competing statements, not a settled harmonization

The court describes Bayoumi and Morgan giving two-minute versus thirty-minute conversation accounts. It also reports Morgan’s declared belief that the encounter was coincidental, and Bayoumi’s denied offer of help beside a quoted MPS account of offering contact/help.

**Record type / scope:** Source content checked; historical inference explicitly bounded

**Context / limit:** BIN DON and Morgan must not be silently equated or counted as two independent witnesses without an identity/alias crosswalk. The court’s whole-record jurisdiction inference remains stronger than any one isolated inconsistency.

**Next record/test:** Join the exact interview/deposition editions and witness identities; preserve two/thirty minutes and the coincidence belief even if a larger inference is supported.

**Citations:** [pinned entry S009](CITATIONS.md#local-2f5bda3a8a7038dc); [Court source 03-md-01570 11182.pdf](https://www.nysd.uscourts.gov/sites/default/files/2025-08/03-md-01570%2011182.pdf); Locator: Physical pp17–18 Pls Ex120 402:10–20, 725:14–726:16, 726:23–727:2 Ex112 97:15–98:4; Ex92 paragraph19; Ex450 193:11–16

<a id="s010"></a>
### S010 — Substantive preliminary judicial findings should not be minimized

The court’s August 28, 2025 decision made an evidence-based jurisdiction assessment supporting an inference of knowing or deliberately indifferent Saudi-linked assistance. It found the encounters more likely than chance and denied the renewed jurisdictional dismissal motion.

**Record type / scope:** Source content checked; historical inference explicitly bounded

**Context / limit:** This was not mere acceptance of pleaded allegations. It also was not a final merits judgment that the Saudi government ordered the attacks; the opinion expressly reserves disputed credibility/meaning questions for trial. No present appellate status is claimed here.

**Next record/test:** Test the cited exhibits and competing explanations on their actual contents, while tracking the procedural standard separately.

**Citations:** [pinned entry S010](CITATIONS.md#local-cec8adb30947e73f); [Court source 03-md-01570 11182.pdf](https://www.nysd.uscourts.gov/sites/default/files/2025-08/03-md-01570%2011182.pdf); Locator: Physical pp6–7,40–45; especially40–42 and45

<a id="s011"></a>
### S011 — The same court rejected dramatic video inferences

The court did not accept that the apartment gathering video established an attack-preparation welcome party or that the Washington video established terrorist reconnaissance; its assistance inference rested on other record evidence.

**Record type / scope:** Source content checked; historical inference explicitly bounded

**Context / limit:** That is an important negative result alongside the jurisdictional findings. This review has not independently examined the complete original videos or adopted the court’s description as a certified audiovisual analysis.

**Next record/test:** Obtain complete original recordings and transcripts before converting selected scenes into intent claims.

**Citations:** [pinned entry S011](CITATIONS.md#local-59abd7345f750224); [Court source 03-md-01570 11182.pdf](https://www.nysd.uscourts.gov/sites/default/files/2025-08/03-md-01570%2011182.pdf); Locator: Physical p42, section C

<a id="s012"></a>
### S012 — The airplane drawing is a precise missing-exhibit lead

The court discusses an airplane drawing and equations as supporting its knowledge inference but also records a dispute over the drawing’s meaning. It identifies Pls Ex678M, MPS696_1–7.

**Record type / scope:** Source content checked; historical inference explicitly bounded

**Context / limit:** Neither this inventory nor a press image establishes the original page sequence, date of creation, complete context, mathematical purpose or use in attack preparation. The drawing was not independently acquired here.

**Next record/test:** Obtain the actual exhibit before analyzing the mathematics or asserting target-specific intent.

**Citations:** [pinned entry S012](CITATIONS.md#local-e33ac6418224825b); [Court source 03-md-01570 11182.pdf](https://www.nysd.uscourts.gov/sites/default/files/2025-08/03-md-01570%2011182.pdf); Locator: Physical pp41,44–45; Pls Ex678M MPS696_1–7

<a id="s013"></a>
### S013 — The EC’s telephone paragraph contains its own timing tension

Internal p14 first describes five Bayoumi–Sudairy calls as occurring while the hijackers were with Bayoumi in San Diego, then lists three in January before the described restaurant meeting and a later date printed only as 02/2000. Both descriptions are preserved.

**Record type / scope:** Source content checked; historical inference explicitly bounded

**Context / limit:** This could be scope shorthand or an editorial problem, but neither explanation was demonstrated. The narrative does not itself supply the call records or content. Do not silently fill the incomplete date with a guessed day.

**Next record/test:** Rebuild the dated call chronology from original records, preserving direction, missing entries and uncertainty.

**Citations:** [pinned entry S013](CITATIONS.md#local-a70bccbfe2ca9a1e); [www.govinfo.gov — GOVPUB-J1_14-PURL-gpo159739.pdf](https://www.govinfo.gov/content/pkg/GOVPUB-J1_14-PURL-gpo159739/pdf/GOVPUB-J1_14-PURL-gpo159739.pdf); Locator: Physical p15 / internal p14; compare physical p9 meeting on/about02/01/2000

<a id="s014"></a>
### S014 — Catalog date conflict and file-version comparison

GovInfo’s abstract says the interview was November12–13,2013, while its title and the EC identify November2015. The GovInfo and FBI-hosted PDFs have different whole-file hashes/sizes, but all17 tested page content streams, image payloads, decoded RGB pixels and page boxes match.

**Record type / scope:** Source content checked; historical inference explicitly bounded

**Context / limit:** The catalog date remains a preserved mismatch; no edit cause is assigned. Matching page components limit the demonstrated file-version difference in this comparison, not the truth of the report. Two hosts supply one documentary chain.

**Next record/test:** Use the original report and both catalog values when requesting the source interview; do not treat a catalog typo hypothesis as an established explanation.

**Citations:** [pinned entry S014](CITATIONS.md#local-9893c6e8e5cfa60a); [www.govinfo.gov — GOVPUB-J1_14-PURL-gpo159739](https://www.govinfo.gov/app/details/GOVPUB-J1_14-PURL-gpo159739); Locator: Catalog Abstract versus title EC physical p3 synopsis source-version-comparison-v3.json

<a id="sf009"></a>
### SF009 — Executive restriction and witness immunity were distinct stages

The JointInquiry appendix says FBI informed it Aug8,2002 of the informant contacts, then refused repeated interview requests and service of congressional deposition/subpoena despite knowing the location. It attributes support to the AttorneyGeneral andAdministration and records a stated concern about future informant recruitment. After an agreed written-question approach, counsel declined responses and said testimony required immunity.

**Context / limit:** Committee account, not the original FBI/AG approval or counsel letter. Exact refusal dates/individual signers and genuine reasons remain unverified. No interview-access completeness, legality or criminal obstruction determination is made.

**Next record/test:** Obtain repeated requests, deposition/subpoena/service instructions, FBI recruitment-risk assessment, AG/Administration concurrence, written interrogatories/delivery and counsel response. Compare dates and stated grounds before assigning individual knowledge or payment motive.

<a id="sf010"></a>
### SF010 — The inquiry retained support and testimony while identifying concrete access limits

Appendix introduction acknowledges substantial agency support and large record access. Informant passage says FBI personnel did testify, while direct informant access was denied. Other sections distinguish review-without-copy restrictions, some allowed verbatim notes, interview monitoring/prebriefing and agreed court/grandjury limitations, including some later resolutions.

**Context / limit:** Do not flatten into no investigation/no information or universally lawful secrecy. Agency prebriefing concern is not evidence every witness was coached to lie; no anonymous former agent is identified as the handler. Restrictions in2002 are not proven current2026 restrictions.

**Next record/test:** Match each restriction to exact decision/record and consequence, preserve allowed access and later resolutions, and test whether a specific missing original changed the conclusion.

## October 30: fifteen additive source findings

These entries preserve the owning source observations, contrary evidence and remaining originals. All earlier 355 source objects are retained unchanged. Source-level interpretation or a conditional calculation does not establish historical execution, recipient receipt, intent or collapse cause.

<a id="fa044"></a>
### FA044 — Conditional Mineta before/after ordering and room/event identity

Mineta's reported exchange before the Pentagon impact, the Commission's room-entry account after impact, and the assumption that the exchange occurred after that same room entry cannot all hold for the same events. The source study checked this conditional ordering in two ways.

**Context / contrary evidence:** Room/event identity is an assumption; recollection is not a calibrated clock. Relaxing a relation permits compatible orders. This does not identify a stand-down order or complete the original interval/clock test.

**Open TODO:** Original room/movement, communication and independent timing records; preserve Mineta's uncertainty about the order and aide.

**Citations:** [owning report](CITATIONS.md#reference-6fc41a6ccb02c3b5); [preserved finding object](data/source-findings.json); [claim/passage ledger](CITATIONS.md#reference-15bb6aa51c18ed98); [original next-record criteria](CITATIONS.md#reference-67cc752ba4d76160)

<a id="nr070"></a>
### NR070 — Enacted2007executive-summary release scope and requiredredaction-reasonsannex

The historical 2007 enactment required public release of a declassified version of the executive summary and separately required a classified congressional annex explaining redactions. It did not require public release of the entire report.

**Context / contrary evidence:** A statutory requirement does not establish that the annex was created, sent or received. The annex provision has no separately stated 30-day deadline, and this source pass did not determine current legal applicability or complete compliance.

**Open TODO:** Actual annex approval/transmittal/congressional receipt, corresponding summary and redaction map, original release and retention records.

**Citations:** [owning report](CITATIONS.md#reference-78e58ca5e1d19391); [preserved finding object](data/source-findings.json); [claim/passage ledger](CITATIONS.md#reference-223caa04458b925a); [original next-record criteria](CITATIONS.md#reference-909704ab7bc3d811)

<a id="nr071"></a>
### NR071 — Committee account of2005–2007release requests, proposed redactions and challenged grounds

The committee report describes a dated 2005–2007 disclosure dispute, including actual replies, a proposed redacted summary, refusals and a challenge to stated grounds.

**Context / contrary evidence:** Partial committee quotations are not complete signed correspondence or a finding of unlawful classification. Replies must not be described as wholly ignored requests; a proposed redacted version is not automatically adequate or approved.

**Open TODO:** Complete requests/replies, attached proposed summary, redaction advice/version history and recipient receipts.

**Citations:** [owning report](CITATIONS.md#reference-09bcbdb9297479d4); [preserved finding object](data/source-findings.json); [claim/passage ledger](CITATIONS.md#reference-c11c8689ea44a96c); [original next-record criteria](CITATIONS.md#reference-39fe0bbfc1635bb9)

<a id="nr072"></a>
### NR072 — CIR footnote20 versus systemic30: visible internal denominator inconsistency

The same CIA report's footnote says 20 respondents while giving category counts 8, 10 and 12; its explicitly cross-referenced systemic section gives those counts with 30 respondents. Under the natural disjoint reading the counts sum to 30.

**Context / contrary evidence:** An editing error is a straightforward possibility; raw question/cohort/coding history is missing. This is distinct from the earlier 14-of-24 versus 30-group comparison, which remains unpooled. It establishes no actual FBI receipt or motive.

**Open TODO:** Original questions, respondent/coding worksheets, report drafts and any erratum. Preserve the printed 20 and 30 without silently repairing either.

**Citations:** [owning report](CITATIONS.md#reference-2d5f8164adb8ef8d); [preserved finding object](data/source-findings.json); [claim/passage ledger](CITATIONS.md#reference-37a3f00e76b33749); [original next-record criteria](CITATIONS.md#reference-a92c4c312aa6582a)

<a id="pl026"></a>
### PL026 — Prague public-statement/final qualifications and unverified edit cause

The public staff statement's rejection of the alleged Prague meeting becomes a more explicitly qualified adverse evidentiary conclusion in the final report, including limits on inferring a person's location from use of a phone.

**Context / contrary evidence:** The final text does not endorse the meeting. The criticized intermediate draft, comments response, edit history and reason remain unacquired; no false attribution or deliberate edit purpose is established.

**Open TODO:** Actual intermediate draft/reviewer response, original travel/phone/identity evidence and underlying interviews.

**Citations:** [owning report](CITATIONS.md#reference-741f25a11bea1917); [preserved finding object](data/source-findings.json); [claim/passage ledger](CITATIONS.md#reference-f7045cb3c9506703); [original next-record criteria](CITATIONS.md#reference-01f0230dabf27eb2)

<a id="pl027"></a>
### PL027 — Preliminary planning attribution, differing event dates and layered knowledge

The preliminary planning statement explicitly identifies dependence on interrogation reporting and particular participants. Some apparently different approval/recruitment dates describe potentially different events; other inconsistent KSM accounts are expressly reported.

**Context / contrary evidence:** The disclosure of dependence weighs against claiming it was entirely hidden. Repeated Commission publications are not independent operational witnesses; source disclosure does not validate voluntariness, translation or private-event chronology.

**Open TODO:** Actual dated sessions/reports, questions and translations, source-access/contamination history and independently generated meeting/communication evidence.

**Citations:** [owning report](CITATIONS.md#reference-0fb6783252bda659); [preserved finding object](data/source-findings.json); [claim/passage ledger](CITATIONS.md#reference-ec0d701977f10dd1); [original next-record criteria](CITATIONS.md#reference-5f26c0aa750a0d8e)

<a id="nr073"></a>
### NR073 — Distributor item6593and qualified film cue locators

A distributor's 52-minute item and indexed timed transcript provide qualified locators for selected impact, exercise and WTC 7 claims.

**Context / contrary evidence:** This is a partial publisher/index representation, not an acquired audiovisual master or concordance across Loose Change editions. Caption times are not historical clock anchors, and allegations are not validated by locating them.

**Open TODO:** Exact eligible audiovisual edition, complete context and speaker/on-screen attribution, with original-source tests for the underlying claims.

**Citations:** [owning report](CITATIONS.md#reference-7f84d65902ced749); [preserved finding object](data/source-findings.json); [claim/passage ledger](CITATIONS.md#reference-ede247f91b278683); [original next-record criteria](CITATIONS.md#reference-7aced604fd89aef1)

<a id="pl028"></a>
### PL028 — Controlled OCR recovers a numeric citation but does not identify the captured-message record

Controlled OCR located a numeric citation that the earlier text layer missed. The newly inspected row connects 14139 to a hotel/NJ-302 context, not directly to the captured-message identity previously associated with that number.

**Context / contrary evidence:** A suffix match alone proves neither identical nor different records, and a parent record may cover multiple topics. OCR of all pages is not full visual reading; negative keyword results are method-specific.

**Open TODO:** Actual case/subserial/office/date/version crosswalk, NJ-302 and Boston EC, attachments and Commission receipt/citation records.

**Citations:** [owning report](CITATIONS.md#reference-d1bca7dfb2162fb8); [preserved finding object](data/source-findings.json); [claim/passage ledger](CITATIONS.md#reference-002fc816b9b9e428); [original next-record criteria](CITATIONS.md#reference-2f244d27e29cbf36)

<a id="pl029"></a>
### PL029 — Composite footnote requires qualification of our FBI source allocation

A composite footnote follows both travel and message assertions. The source pass qualifies our earlier overly specific allocation of individual FBI references, while retaining the separate explicit CIA capture-report citation and a contrary account of the code terms.

**Context / contrary evidence:** Travel relevance is plausible, not proved exclusive allocation. No raw message or false-citation conclusion follows. The printed pre-capture interrogation-date problem remains unresolved and must not be silently corrected.

**Open TODO:** Exact cited versions and workpapers, raw messages/translation/code key, sessions/questions, custody and original receipt records.

**Citations:** [owning report](CITATIONS.md#reference-15c0450ec9020473); [preserved finding object](data/source-findings.json); [claim/passage ledger](CITATIONS.md#reference-854d76e911aa4a22); [original next-record criteria](CITATIONS.md#reference-2a4a9c8117261856)
